ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e8304	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, I would also like to expressly include the Secretary of State. Estimated antagonists! Dear friends and dear friends! It's time for me – time to leave the political stage. I've been thinking for a long time. What can I do to you, what can I say to you in my last speech? Farewelling speeches are often used for a list of their own successes. This then becomes relatively quickly a self-throwed Laudatio. I want to try to keep this relatively short. But I'm not coming out. I would like to report briefly on my work as a minister: the increase in budgets for the free scene and the art scholarships, the strengthening of contemporary art, successful staff decisions – Martin Kušej in the Burgtheater, Bogdan Rošcić in the Staatsoper, Stella Rollig in Belvedere. Commissioner Thun-Hohenstein managed for the first time to show a single female art position at the Venice Biennial. To tell of my work as a Member of Parliament is ultimately to report on many requests, many requests to museums, media and machinations. Of course you also have to tell about the defeats, and they were also numerous. I then have to report probably or badly on the failure of the Information Freedom Act and also on the fact that I did not provide modern media promotion. Of course, such a farewell speech is always an opportunity to open up the great perspective of looking at the world's gap: Should we talk about climate change, about the state of democracy in times of the pandemic, about major issues such as freedom of the press, and about current processes such as the current spell of the “Wiener Zeitung” in the backrooms? Or should one complain of the decay of the political craftsmanship, the domination of the PR shows, the predicament of distractions that are driven through the media village like the proverbial Sau, the first-arming of right-wing populism and right-wing extremism, or at the end – which, admittedly, always brings joy – hand over once more the political opponents, the competitors and competitors, as they are called euphemistic, even verbally? No, I'm not doing anything about it. I firmly believe in the meaning of good debates and good culture of conversation. The contradiction tightens your own arguments. Liberal democracy needs the respectful debate rather than the moralizing permanence. I want to focus on it myself, and I begin at this point with an excuse with the political counterparts, with journalists, with companions and away companions, whom I may have offended in the zeal of the – especially social media – fight. Unfortunately, that's not all right. Having said this, from my point of view, it is only more about two things I would like to say here: first of all, thank you – it is my duty and choice to say thank you. It was a privilege for me to work for Franz Vranitzky. It was a privilege to serve as ministers and delegates. I thank my wife Isabella, who has endured me in these five years, with my close personal friends. I thank the parliamentary staff and the staff in the SPÖ, the press. I would like to thank the members of the cabinet, and I am particularly grateful to the Social Democracy, which has made it possible for me to take a course. I would also like to thank the officials of the Federal Chancellor's Office, in particular the Constitutional Service and the Art Section, with which I worked very intensively. Speaking of art: I have always understood myself as a advocate of art and thus come to my heart's theme. Please really look at the artists in our country! They perform great and just spend difficult times. Culture is not just a lip confession or a tourist attraction. Culture is to talk to Antonio Gramsci – I quote – owners of “your own personality”. It serves the – quote – “winning a higher consciousness by which one can understand one’s own historical value, one’s own function in life, one’s own rights and obligations”. Without artists, we live in a world that is silent, external and foreign to us. Art and culture are powerful resonance axes. Many of you know my favorite quote, I can't spare you today. It's from sociologist and philosopher Hartmut Rosa. He has written a book on resonances, and he defines the resonance as the longing “for a world that answers it. ” This resonance is a term that of course has a lot to do with art and music, and thus a life without literature, theatre, music – in short without art – is barbaric. I would remind you, ladies and gentlemen: The cinemas are dark. In the theatres, lonely actors try out, on the dance surfaces the dust lies. Please, let's think about whether those who had to lock up first must also be those who are the last to lock up again! I do not want to take up the areas against each other, but what is allowed to believers in the churches and consumers in the trade should also be approved of the people who love the dance, the theatre, the music, the concerts. [[We welcome SPÖ and NEOS, members of FPÖ and Greens, and MEPs Engelberg and Niss.]] As a politician, I didn't want to leave the stage without at least giving the piece a small turn to the better: if not the epochal throw, then a good turn, a useful, successful contribution. My political work has nothing revolutionary: weighting, balancing interests, finding compromises – all that in the consciousness that one always has twice in life all of this in the consciousness of the value of liberal democracy; all of this with a lack of touch on lifestyle issues that were discussed so intensively and passionately by my person; all of this always in the light of the responsibility for our country, just as I learned at the time as a 27-year-old by Franz Vranitzky; all of this in the spirit of social democratic values freedom, equality, justice and solidarity. However, I believe in a revolution, and that is the revolution after Joseph Beuys – by the way, there is a very remarkable exhibition in the Belvedere. I quote: “The only revolutionary force is human creativity. The only revolutionary force is art." Dear Ladies and gentlemen, dear friends, good for you, for your political work! Thank you for the cooperation, for the dispute, for the heights and depths. I allow myself to end with a repetition as advocate for art: Please take good care of the artists!

Dear friends of the parliamentary delegation! “Who cares? What crisis?” – This is the title of a piece at the Vorarlberger Landestheater. ==References== The Landestheater plays at 5 p.m., because starting from 8 p.m., they play since it's possible, and it's a full house. Of course, we would like to have that everywhere. It is the most difficult thing for artists not to be able to perform, not to be able to appear, not to show what their art is. ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e9305	Members of Parliament are hereby informed of the matter. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e9326	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! The voluntary social year offers young adults aged 18 to 24 the opportunity to work ten, eleven months in social, non-profit institutions in Austria. In 2020, it completed around 1,500 volunteers, 85 percent of them women and only 15 percent men. They have been trained specifically for working with older people, with children, with people with special needs or in difficult situations. Operations were mainly rescue organizations such as the Red Cross, the Samaritan League or the Caritas. Over 95 percent of the participants in the Red Cross completed their voluntary social year in the rescue service. The motivation for this is diverse. In addition to the classical aspect, namely social engagement, participants are trying to orient themselves to a professional orientation. They want to gain practical experience or they want to bridge a waiting year and use it for training. In addition, there is also the possibility for men to be credited for civil service. A voluntary social year also contributes to the formation of personality and, last but not least, to the achievement of key qualifications for later careers. 75 percent of the graduates of a voluntary social year remain in this sector, then continue to work in it, or begin a training, a study in the social or medical field. To inspire young people for culture, yes, that is also a great concern for us. Yes, it is a must for our society, but this should start earlier – not only with 18 – in kindergarten, in school and finally in the parents' house. After that, nothing stands in the way of voluntary volunteering in cultural associations. On the contrary: It's an important facet in our culture. Since we are also working on fair pay because it is anchored in the government programme and we want to achieve improvements in the remuneration in the arts and culture sector, this opening would be to work for pocket money reward only quite again, I think, only counterproductive. Summary: The commitment of young people to culture is an important goal of our party. Cultural education must start early. This is contrary to the fact that the Corona crisis has also shown us how important voluntary support in the social field is precisely that the labour market situation in the social field is strained and needs cannot always be met, so ultimately voluntary workers are needed. That is why we are sorry, but we cannot support this request to extend the voluntary social year to the cultural field. Thank you.

Today we are discussing the Land Labor Law 2021 at lunchtime, and this is undoubtedly a day of joy for all the institutions that have worked on this project, and a day of joy for about 35 000 employees and about 10,000 employers throughout Austria. What's this about? – We have managed to bring nine national laws into a federal law and thus into federal competence. There are now no more 100 regulations governing this issue throughout Austria, but 20. We have managed to implement more administrative simplification in Austria and also for the workers concerned, especially for those working somewhere in a border area and, so to speak, once in Lower Austria and then working in Upper Austria a month later, to increase legal certainty. – A great thank you to all the ladies and gentlemen who participated in this project. I would like to stress that these are really very regional situations in the sense of communities located in the neighborhood in which the farms are located, or, if it is defined a little larger, in the sense of districts that are adjacent. ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e10600	President. — I call the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats). Dr. Martin Kocher. - Here you go, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e10624	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! In addition to the acute crisis reduction and addressing the consequences of this health crisis for the labour market, it is also important to create a basic framework that will allow employment in different sectors, create employment and also simplify procedures. A sector in which this desire was particularly great is the area of agriculture and land labour law. What is the status quo? – There is a fundamental law at the federal level, nine implementing laws of the countries and over 100 regulations. This, of course, leads to certain problems of a bureaucratic nature, including problems at the national borders, which is why it was particularly important to me – together with the Minister for Agriculture – as a simplification. ==References== The Single Land Labour Act is now to enter into force in July. It replaces these nine country labour regulations and reduces the number of regulations from 100 to about 20. This benefits more than 30 000 agricultural workers and potentially 160 000 agricultural and forestry farms in Austria. I too would like to thank all those who have made progress on this project before my time – the social partners, my predecessors and many parliamentarians. I think it's a big reform step. What does he mean? – It means a sustainable and clear debureaucratisation and simplification of the legal situation for agricultural workers. It or the package allows employers' mergers in order to jointly set up businesses in a region in such a way that work can be divided flexibly and thus create attractive, sustainable and sustainable employment conditions in seasonal agriculture and forestry. In addition, of course, it is also about things that have not yet been mentioned, for example, an improved and uniform regulation as regards the compatibility of work and family. There were very different agricultural labour regulations in the different Länder. Now it is clear that there is – uniformly – the papal month, the full recognition of parental care on all service-related claims and also the right to care for care. This creates clear conditions, from which both businesses and employees in the industry benefit, and in addition, we also allow small and highly seasonal enterprises to provide employment opportunities and create additional jobs with the new work combinations. Overall, this is a major reform project in my view, ensuring that bureaucracy is simplified, by which we promote employment and create overall better framework conditions for both sides: both for employees and for businesses. Thank you.

There have been companies that have had business agreements on home office, which have also worked. There have already been provisions in the field of accident insurance. There has also always been discussions as to whether employers actually have to make the resources available to them. The workers' chamber was always there for it, and others were more controversial. Now very many of these questions are simply clarified, and this clarity, this legal certainty for people in the home office is a very important, a very essential step. There is no question at all that we welcome this package and will also agree to this package. [[Congratulations with the Greens and representatives of the ÖVP.]] Of course, a number of questions remain open. Mr Loacker referred to mobile work only from one place. Is that really the final conclusion of wisdom? – Not necessarily, we need to think about it. I would have preferred it personally if, for example, home office arrangements were to be concluded on the basis of a business agreement, because In my opinion, collective regulations are always better and stronger than individual ones, but we now have at least the opportunity to do both. In particular, it will also be a matter of observing closely what reinforced home office actually means, whether people who are in the home office are sufficiently involved in the business as a whole, in business social benefits, in business training – not that a group is again depended on compared to the others. Last but not least: Home Office will work if we create at home a working environment that is not characterized by home learning, home education, and, in reality, care. Home office should not be understood as a means of privatising services previously provided to the public. This may be necessary in pandemic times in the short term, but we will have to ensure appropriate social policy conditions in the future, which will actually ensure that home office is work and not hidden care. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e11763	Mr President. High house! Of course, the pandemic Home Office and the work of home have received an increased attention. Much was discussed about how this should and can be designed. On the basis of surveys, we know that many people who now have experience working from home want to continue in a hybrid form – not only from home, but partly from home and partly from the office – but also from home. It was therefore particularly important to us that there be the possibility of individual solutions at the operational level to address the different requirements in the various sectors. The 2021 Home Office Package provides a framework for the labour, tax and social security measures that are possible and necessary in connection with HomeOffice. We address the needs of both sides – this is very important to me – the side of businesses and the side of workers. Of course – it has already been mentioned – the social partners and the industrial associations were involved in the preparation to create a regulatory framework which represents a first major step. Of course, digitalization has been greatly accelerated by the pandemic. Experts assume that we have experienced a digital shift of almost ten years in a year if we compare this with a situation without a pandemic. This means that further developments will also be needed when working from home. That is why it was so important to me that there is a relatively rapid evaluation of the measures, based on experience in practice – I think that is crucial – to make adjustments, and this is very soon when we speak of 2022. Which points does the Home Office package include? – A definition of home office, then continues to establish that Homeoffice is an agreement matter, which, I believe, is also very important. There is no obligation to the home office, no one can be forced, and that, I believe, is a good condition. Of course I'd like to say, That it is now important during the pandemic that all those who can use this – both businesses and employees – home office as broad as possible to limit contacts. A second important point, of course, is the financial framework. It is clear that employers must provide the means of work. If this is not the case, an appropriate lump sum for the own resources shall be granted. We thus ensure that Homeoffice does not represent an additional financial burden for employees. Then we still have worker protection in our home office: There too, I think, it was important to make it clear that all the regulations applicable to work in the office, such as rest periods and working hours, have also been valid in the home office. The rules can therefore be trusted on the part of workers. There is an explicit ban on private dwellings for labour inspectorates, unless there is an invitation from the employee or the employee to do so. Finally, accident insurance is also clarified once again. Work accidents in the home office are insured. But also accidents that happen along the way, for example by the way from home office to kindergarten or school, when you bring the children there. Overall, it is a wealth of measures and a sustainable regulatory framework from my point of view, which has been a huge concern for all of us to ensure planning security even after the pandemic. I believe that this is also a matter in which both sides – workers on the one hand, and businesses on the other – can gain and, by doing so, there are economic opportunities to make jobs more attractive. I would like to thank all those who have contributed.

Accurate financing gaps in the area of bad weather compensation are corrected by retranslations, thus securing financing in this area. As of 2022, the financing will be systematically changed. The payments of the Pension Insurance Institute and the federal social security contribution will be completely suspended, in return, 13 million euros will flow from labour market policy resources into bad weather compensation stoppers each year. After nearly 18 years of resuming, a very good solution has now been found, which can actually be described as a win-win result for service providers and service providers in the construction sector. Construction workers are exposed to the weather. This leads to interruptions of working conditions during the winter. In this sector, the employer is also often changed. In this BUAG pot, both the construction worker and the employer pay the salary, and the State subsidizes this special scheme. Each employee thereby acquires the so-called bridge money, which can then be used in different forms. Either at the end of a professional life can be drawn out of this pot and one uses this bridge money or those who like to continue to work can consume part of it as an additional premium; that is, it is a safety net and an incentive system at the same time. Belakovich.]] I would like to thank the Federal Minister and his team, who have seen all of this and have been able to work out some of it. There is a definition of persons with a lower epidemiological risk for themselves and others, as the explanations clearly show. Four points are defined, four circumstances under which a person may have such a lower risk: Vaccination has been mentioned; it also includes a undergone infection, but also a negative evidence of pathogen with antigen test or PCR and proven immunity. The fourth point is also very important, since there will certainly be a number of scientific issues to be clarified, but this is an example of how regulations can refer to the current state of science and can be properly implemented. Also very, very important: It's not about being privileged or discriminated against. The thing is another one. The normal state is that there is no limitation. Restrictions can only be justified by reason of a reasonable risk. The question then arises to what extent people who meet one of these four criteria can be justified and to what extent further limit them. That is why it is a fundamental right question because we want to look specifically at it, because ultimately we all want as much freedom as possible and only as much restriction as necessary. It creates a clear framework for it to be responsibly transposed into regulations if necessary. I ask for broad consent. - Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e17079	Mr President. Federal Minister! (Parliament adopted the legislative resolution) Ladies and gentlemen in front of the screens! Today it is not only about the Epidemic Act and the COVID-19 Act, no, we are also negotiating the COVID-19 Emission Grant Act. Since the beginning of the pandemic, the Federal Government has made a contribution to the countries and the municipalities in the amount of their additional expenses due to the pandemic from the Covid-19 crisis management fund. These include, among other things, the costs of protective equipment, personnel expenditure, health shooting, the provision of infrastructure and so on. With today's debate on the amendment to the COVID-19 Emissions Grant Act, the pandemic-related additional costs of public pharmacies and, as Mr Diesner-Wais has already said, rescue and health transport services will now be paid off from the federal government. In the case of public pharmacies, it concerns in particular the additional burden by the free implementation of the Covid-19 tests and the free distribution of antigen tests for own use. In the case of rescue and health transport services, the Covid-19-related additional expenditure is mainly related to additional expenditure arising from the transport of Covid-19 suspected cases and from persons suffering from Covid-19 who are not taken over by health insurance companies. Special hygiene and disinfection measures also cause additional costs, including staff and protective clothing. These additional expenditures, which constitute a significant financial burden, are largely pre-financed by the rescue agencies from own resources. These infrastructures – pharmacies, rescue and health services – are especially important to all of us in the crisis, we have all noticed this in the last 12 to 13 months. They are widely available, both in the city and in rural areas of Austria, and essentially for the Covid fight. This item is therefore intended to ensure the financial support of the Infrastructures are also so important, and I thank you in advance for your majority approval.

Now, in Austria, most of the bank customers hold a current account, in which it is usually “included” – under quotation marks – but that means it is not free either. If you have a current account and you pay a flat-rate fee, you will usually also pay for your cash withdrawals. So it's not free now, you have to say. Now, in principle, it's about finding a solution, because it could be that the banks – now they're practically voluntarily calculating nothing – will say tomorrow: We now calculate 30 cents per cash collection. – That would be legally covered. This problem should and must be solved very quickly. There is also another subject. This has now been experienced in the times of Corona, when suddenly the official means of payment, namely cash, has been rejected by some super-klugens. Some restaurants, some businesses, have refused to take cash, with the flawed argument – that was eh Fakenews – that the coronaviruses can be transmitted via cash. So you have to be very, very careful in this area. That is, as FPÖ, we want consumers in Austria to have access to cash, which is close to space and continues to have free access to it. The National Disability Action Plan is based on the UN Convention on the Rights of People with Disabilities, which was ratified by Austria in 2008. This one sees in art. 33 para. 1 the establishment of a general government coordination mechanism. This includes setting up a focal point – as we know, at the federal level, this is the Federal Ministry of Social Affairs – as well as the creation of a national action plan. The first NAP – I am now shortening – was created for the period 2012 to 2021 and includes 250 measures. 2013, i.e. one year after the introduction of the NAP, after Austria's first state examination, there were the closing observations, which refer to the necessary steps to be taken in further succession. A small excursion at this point to the closing observations: A total of 58 recommendations have been made for Austria. For example, it is noted that urgent steps must be taken to ensure inclusive education for all children and adults with disabilities. Furthermore, a participatory preparation and monitoring of the National Action Plan on Disability and the adaptation of the state and the concept of people with disabilities will be carried out – not: People with special needs, special needs, but: People with disabilities – recommended as important steps for Austria to the social model of disability according to the UN Disability Convention. In 2019, the evaluation of the first NAP was commissioned. The University of Vienna has carried it out and will also be published in 2020. At the last meeting of the Human Rights Committee, we all made a request for the second NAP to be drawn up and implemented in 2022 and will run until 2030. Why? ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e23180	Dr. Johannes Margreiter is speaking. - Please, ladies and gentlemen.
ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e23204	Mr President. Thank you, Mrs. Federal Minister! High house! Now we need to clarify something: We are talking here about a change in the Federal Roads Toll Act, which concerns only the driving power dependent toll. This applies only to vehicles over 3.5 tonnes – only to ensure that the toll is reduced by 75 percent for electric vehicles or hydrogen vehicles. We change this number in the law, but the composition of the driving power-dependent toll – we could hold a private body – is a highly complex process in which three components play a role: the infrastructure component, the noise component and the air component; and only in the area of the infrastructure component can it be possible for electric vehicles and hydrogen vehicles with fuel cell drive to reduce the toll – or this component, this infrastructure component – by 75 percent instead of 50 percent so far. This is a small contribution to the energy transition or to the mobility turnaround, but will not trigger the world. We NEOS will agree, but with a little bit Abdominal pain, because we would very much argue that we operate this decarbonisation, which is aimed at achieving the objective of technologically neutral. The exit from the combustion engine can't be self-evident. If today a combustion engine causes similar CO2 emissions, namely zero direction, as is the case in the case of electric mobility, then there is nothing against further investment in combustion technology. Synthetic fuels are being developed that are CO2-neutral. In addition, I am surprised – and I say that also with regard to hydrogen – that this proposal for a law speaks of the fact that only the fuel cell technology of this privilege can be reduced by up to 75 percent, because the hydrogen combustion engine is also a hydrogen technology that is CO2-neutral. So we would like to see technology neutrality in the future. The goal cannot be the evaporation of the combustion engine, but the goal must be to defuse CO2. [[Congratulations on MEPs from NEOS, ÖVP and Greens.]] If this is possible without the combustion engine being eliminated, then we should go this way. In this sense, I am very close to this open letter, which has been sent to the Federal Chancellor today, in which it is about distanceing us from the demand for an annual number of years to which the technology of the internal combustion engine will finally come out. We share and support the arguments in there. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e24970	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen! Thank you, Mrs. Minister! Yes, the report shows that domestic environmental promotion is one of the most important instruments we have in climate protection. Environmental support provides budget resources, support for private, for businesses, for communities so that they can invest in climate protection. We have had this instrument for a very long time, and I wanted to thank the staff at the Ministry of Climate Change, but also in the CCP, who are doing excellent work in the implementation of this environmental promotion. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Gödl.]] The report concerns three years, 2017 to 2019. As almost EUR 423 million was paid out in grants, and as successful as that was, the problem in recent years was that there was simply too little money for the individual funding programmes. You know that from the out-of-oil and gas promotion: Soon there was this promotion, she was already out of print. The people who had the fastest Internet connection have got the promotion, the rest has gone out empty. Since the entry of the Greens into the Federal Government, the fundamental change has been made. We have been able to put in a climate turbo together with our coalition partner, and if we look at it now, we see: This year, we have seven times as much budget appropriations for oil-rehabilitation and heating as we have in the budget proposal for 2019. [[Congratulation with the Greens and the Abg. Gödl.]] This is seven times more money for people who want to change to clean heating! When we consider what kind of budget we decided last year – we also had an UFG novelle – we see that this year alone we have more money available for environmental promotion than in the entire three-year reporting period together. I think that can be seen too. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] What we are probably not going to see in the next environmental promotion report is the investment premium that we have jointly decided to disbursement hundreds of millions of euros in budget appropriations, which were not quoted as funding, but which, in principle, went exactly the same way, namely, that investments by companies in climate protection have been massively funded by 14 percent. What's particularly important to me is: In recent years, the funding policy has also been running a little with the casting can. It was simply promoted without a proper goal and without strategy or with too little strategy. This too is now fundamentally changing. ParlaMint-AT_2021-03-25-027-XXVII-NRSITZ-00091_d8e25663	Mr President. Minister! High house! Ladies and gentlemen! This agenda concerns the ratification of the amended Additional Protocol to the Geneva Air Re Content Convention to protect against heavy metals in the air. Some times you have the feeling of getting rid of things over the air, you get rid of them quickly. It is often not thought much about it. In the case of heavy metals, this is quite a big problem. Heavy metals have the property that they accumulate in the body and become more and more and are not mined. This is, of course, a very serious problem from a health point of view, because it can also cause diseases. In this case, it is also specifically about lead, cadmium, mercury, and that is to be reduced. In the case of petrol, lead was banned. That worked very well. Now there are a lot of areas where you still have to do this to get the heavy metals out of here. They are used for corrosion protection, for example, in the case of colours. Heavy metals can also be found in the children's toys. That's where we really should try to reduce it. They often emit refineries, and in the waste burning they are quite a problem. I think that we should really pool our efforts to reduce these outcasts. I believe that even the energy transition – if we consume less oil and so on – will help to relieve refineries, and also the electric cars, which then release less heavy metals when brake, because they brake electrically. That is why I would like to invite everyone to agree now and help reduce heavy metals in the air. Thank you.

It must not be possible for pigeons to fly there where there are already pigeons, in large cities, in large collections where many people live, but it is also about peripheral spaces, where it is more difficult, as Mr Gödl said, to build a district heating plant, because simply the large customers are not there, where it is difficult to survive in villages where few people live. My great plea is therefore that we should take account of these regions too, so that there is no division of society: Those who live in agglomerations are well cared for, and those who live on the edge or in small units cannot participate in this life. [[Congratulations on ODP and FPÖ Members.]] However, my speech also refers in particular to the issue of biodiversity. There have been a lot of debates about this recently, and many do not know what biodiversity is all about. Without wanting to be schoolmasters here: According to the definition, this is the diversity of life forms, i.e. animals, plants, mushrooms, bacteria, the diversity of habitats, and genetic diversity. There you can already see that this is a very complex matter, very comprehensive. She plays in many areas of life. 2010 was the UN year of biodiversity, and at that time a UN Member State conference was held in Japan, Nagoya, where this quoted Nagoya Protocol was adopted. For the first time, there has been a worldwide agreement that all UN Treaty states have agreed to ensure this biodiversity, biodiversity. The ambitious goal was to reduce the losses of biodiversity by 2020, i.e. by last year. There have always been such goals that were far too ambitious because they were in truth not to be achieved. You have to be so realistic. The point was, however, that it was not only about preserving biodiversity, but also about access to genetic resources and the use of these resources. There it was established that biodiversity should also be used economically, for example for medicinal products. Just under the title Access-and-Benefit-Sharing it was about: How can genetic resources be used, for example in the rainforest, so that on the one hand, there is access to them, and on the other hand, those who live there have some of them? This agreement states that everyone should have a fair share of it. This Nagoya protocol was then extended by the so-called Cartagena Protocol and another. The many protocols that fascinate there are a bit confusing, but in the end they should cover all areas of biodiversity protection. The Cartagena Protocol deals with the fact that there is a legally binding contract to protect the environment, to protect human health when living changed organisms are placed on the market. That sounds pretty bulky, sounds a bit dramatic, but should mean: If genetically modified organisms – for which we are not, but happens in the world – are placed on the market, then protection of human beings and the environment should be ensured. There are a number of liability provisions and the like. In this respect, it is important to do so, and in so far as it is important to protect biodiversity on the one hand, but in my opinion, it is also useful when it comes to developing medicines and the like, vaccines. Thank you very much.

In addition, there have been separate Commissions of Inquiry. But what we have done is an examination of how the Court of Auditors conducts it at all museums, along its constitutional standards. We have found massive shortcomings and shortcomings, for example, lack of an overall economic overview. There was a lack of business know-how. We have noted repeated non-compliance with legal requirements in many areas. For example, there were construction measures that did not have a construction permit, but which in particular were not compatible with the military construction guidelines. There were misconceptions in the area of collections. Electronic inventory was only made up of 40 percent, so 60 percent were not recorded. There were misconceptions. There was a lack of compliance with the senior staff. In this respect, there were no corresponding regulations and also instructions for action to be taken by the Ministry. The Ministry's supervision and supervision was not sufficiently observed. In short: There were also inadequate storage conditions within the storage conditions, and there was a lack of implementation of the conflict resolution plan, as recommended by the Lordespsychological Service itself. There's a lot to do. We have made recommendations that this museum needs to be developed in a timely manner, the audited site needs to look at it. I stress that the Ministry of Defence has answered us in the opinion – and this is stated in the report – that it has commissioned the relevant section to analyse and improve without delay the structure and procedures of the military history museum. We recommend to evaluate the suitability of the organizational form, we recommend that we analyze the legal and operational knowledge and also ensure the know-how for the military history museum. – Thank you for your attention.
